{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01RS5d7LZis9zY1Xg5jhiBP5","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Now I have a clear picture!\n\nIn the pci_device_id table (iwl_hw_card_ids), the entries for 0x2526 have many subdevice IDs, BUT there's a catch-all entry at line 599:\n`{IWL_PCI_DEVICE(0x2526, PCI_ANY_ID, iwl9000_trans_cfg)}`\n\nIn the dev_info_table, there are entries for 0x2526 with specific subdevice IDs like:\n- 0x0010, 0x0014, 0x0018, 0x001C (for iwl9260_2ac_160_cfg)\n- 0x6010, 0x6014, 0x8010, 0x8014, 0xA014, 0xE010, 0xE014 (for iwl9260_2ac_160_cfg)\n- 0x0030, 0x0038, 0x003C, 0x4030 (for iwl9560_2ac_160_cfg)\n\nBut looking at the pci_device_id table specifically for 0x2526, I see entries like:\n- 0x0034, 0x0060, 0x0064, 0x00A0, 0x00A4, 0x0210, 0x0214, 0x0230, etc.\n\nBut NOT entries for:\n- 0x0010, 0x0014, 0x0018, 0x001C (the iwl9260_160 OEM/ODM subdevice IDs)\n- 0x6010, 0x6014, 0x8010, 0x8014, 0xA014, 0xE010, 0xE014 (OEM/ODM variants)\n\nThe catch-all PCI_ANY_ID entry means that devices with 0x2526 and ANY subdevice ID will be detected by the PCI layer, but they'll all use the generic iwl9000_trans_cfg instead of the specific 160 MHz config.\n\n\nThe real issue is that these OEM/ODM subdevice IDs need explicit entries in the pci_device_id table paired with their correct device configurations so they load the proper settings instead of falling back to the generic config. The bug report mentions \"0x04010, 0x4018 and 0x401C\" which might be using different notation or there could be a typo in how those IDs are represented.\n\nLooking at the actual bug description more carefully—\"missing PCI subdevice entries for 0x2526 (0x04010, 0x4018 and 0x401C)\"—this appears to be listing subdevice IDs that lack corresponding entries in the device ID table. The fix involves adding these missing entries to iwl_hw_card_ids with their associated device configurations so the driver recognizes them during probe and loads the appropriate settings. The firmware loading fails because devices matching those specific subdevice IDs get caught by the PCI_ANY_ID fallback and load with the generic iwl9000_trans_cfg instead of their correct configuration. The IWL_PCI_DEVICE macro defines:- device: the device ID\n- subdevice: the subdevice/subsystem device ID\n- subvendor: PCI_ANY_ID (allows any subvendor)So when a PCI device is detected, the kernel PCI subsystem looks at:- Vendor ID (Intel)\n- Device ID (0x2526)\n- Subvendor ID\n- Subsystem Device ID\n\nThe pci_device_id table entry with (dev=0x2526, subdev=PCI_ANY_ID) means it will match 0x2526 with ANY subsystem device ID.\n\nBut the entries that ARE explicitly in the pci_device_id table (like 0x2526:0x0034, 0x2526:0x0060, etc.) are more specific matches. If a device comes in as 0x2526:0x0034, it will match the explicit entry (0x2526, 0x0034) with higher priority than the generic (0x2526, PCI_ANY_ID) entry.\n\nNow, the question is: for a device with ID 0x2526 and subdevice ID 0x0010, which entry in the pci_device_id table will match?\n\nLooking at the table:- There's NO explicit entry for (0x2526, 0x0010)\n- There IS a catch-all entry for (0x2526, PCI_ANY_ID)\n\nSo 0x2526:0x0010 will match the PCI_ANY_ID entry, getting iwl9000_trans_cfg.\n\nThen, the dev_info_table is searched:- Line 1034-1037 compares dev_info->device (0x2526) with pdev->device (0x2526) - matches\n- And dev_info->subdevice (0x0010) with pdev->subsystem_device (0x0010) - matches\n\n\n- This finds the matching entry and assigns cfg = iwl9260_2ac_160_cfg. The matching logic at lines 1034-1037 should work correctly—it checks if the device and subdevice IDs match, allowing for wildcards with IWL_CFG_ANY. So the dev_info_table lookup should succeed and override the initial configuration. That seems unlikely for a standard build, but it's worth considering. IWL_CFG_ANY is defined as (~0), which means \"any value\" - a wildcard. So the device/subdevice matching in the dev_info_table lookup at lines 1034-1037 will work correctly for any device.\n\nNow, let me reconsider the whole situation. The dev_info_table has entries for 0x2526 with subdevice IDs 0x0010, 0x0014, etc. The lookup logic should find these and override the cfg. So where's the bug?\n\nActually, wait. Let me look at the order of matching. In the PCI subsystem, when a device is probed, the pci_device_id table is searched in order. The first matching entry is used. So:- A device with 0x2526:0x0010 will be compared against all entries in iwl_hw_card_ids- The first matching entry wins\n\nIf we have:\n1. ...specific entries like (0x2526, 0x0034), (0x2526, 0x0060), etc.\n2. ...then the catch-all (0x2526, PCI_ANY_ID)\n\nFor a device 0x2526:0x0010, it won't match any of the specific entries (because 0x0010 is not in the list), so it will match the catch-all and get iwl9000_trans_cfg.\n\nThen the dev_info_table lookup happens and SHOULD override it with the correct cfg.\n\nSo the fix should work IF the dev_info_table is being checked correctly. But I notice that the dev_info_table entries are guarded by CONFIG_IWLMVM. If this config option is not enabled, the entries won't exist, and the lookup will fail silently, leaving the wrong cfg in place!\n\nSo the possible bug is:1. The pci_device_id table doesn't have explicit entries for the OEM subdevice IDs (0x0010, 0x0014, 0x0018, 0x001C, etc.), so they fall back to PCI_ANY_ID with the generic config\n\n\n2. The dev_info_table entries for these IDs are conditional on CONFIG_IWLMVM being enabled. If that config wasn't set in 5.6-rc2, those override entries simply wouldn't be compiled in, leaving devices stuck with the wrong configuration. The real solution is adding these OEM variants directly to the pci_device_id table so they're recognized properly regardless of build configuration.\n\nThe core issue is clear—these subdevice IDs belong in iwl_hw_card_ids. I'm confident about the fix location and approach, though the exact underlying cause might vary slightly from CONFIG_IWLMVM being disabled.","signature":"EqcVCkYICxgCKkAHyzp4z6bnfp//yhyVJgaXnMWMGsh0Qud3fA8vYFOfWvJQQLwAdAG0kH65VLcmI09FkycJpaVmn/u40T+8m9HbEgzsHkjZbCLr0vEiTVMaDMsCqtcEhOxg6vPVPiIwmm7NpKK2cwqfq9PbtqDB5D7y3TU2ChdZwOBs3EMRwTFXp2/BzyM+KpP7aRYT+dFdKo4UlJ/Fi5miZzwvbtxq64PWEC+om86H0xHXy/f/dz8Si+paDVP5liaJviU/GpgOI4UVMq5VwnGjdZMCaKTfhNot+gRnwDA07ZEhG4md7jebNGbY/DzDc/FxyMtHkgm6dFv/9wepJ2WU0waqFECLLwjwjexzs7sNhfATxf0kdb1B0hcb8uQzTyJbYpmIsq6R00mihQ66Uo810tOR/l8tLESIjW4Q9VRGzbCfoNsjaVUu+c3aibT1H7gg8L8RM9DE7oIK0wP1o5qnQAzHnHn7B+W+Vdw1NC9nNyA7z+cReasZEWFN0l2TcEjnwhuI33JM4gXvowijj8LfT7jg5+8wdbM3jfBFz0ibdeorcjTXyt7kaUF7TJwwTSA+dW7DbKxHCmcyGEdEpvJiOV1rKkVLAl7pv9MXGzGvGsVAE5xMyrXBJF+AJE+5eQ/0a65TEehYqbkfyd+dlPiWCxobAwqDlV3IUNdBjgG8HS5G94OxrlH+0WVI5WAgU9GTRXHrFPkOMsBxFEUJzzt8yuGyrfPLSBohFdZ9fjmr96/olhZ/KnW6LUR575GCx6PLz7TRbKujeUiuTB5KE6Mw++3lx+1YCIbHNJv/JJjft6CP00i2OOEsCDy2ukoVl/61pYKBgsZGd02zYaorh7vsBuWSCtHthEufPtzBj9r9MKbn/lJSWJvgOLeA1p4gtzZATvIisVfSR2qnYjMQEacKq+fW4mLvYY6IIDhzHe/CyhMpZq/P6rm89qVBzeDUZmZQcGqstmFAUnPyZQz6uKLrf8eO/LnG7LxFbNYrY3vRETF1rP2WB+0+h0oKAY5Q8/Ivc7/ksQXB0AMFK+0iU9q/uOekTnaHkJdfvCnIvucLsNqoX15iQcDc/XFu/lva9DMpqoVJLoZi9MPEFA+EI7OVR5weRSD9ZAtHZCxyaUPw7YN3PoyjIrfUbwZJzZEIRBU88jFL8eK5kTo5s+LkgimylyxqODJYwskQU+7uuFDA8y+VbcQX+k8IIOTf4hU6SqJ23TF4b3wjW9IqE6AIUEhjv6y2sQDDK4BWtnC9NFXPld78wlBB0IB5hPgQKfBR5uMnd9GYq4iGe7VNZTRURoVCb80DH4d7o6Dlvn1BOv+R8IZWodNYmGg+ui2Uv+nPECwzovblrKCSwtt80zoKn6+LzlRSPYo4tKrYIv0LpOfemlng3AI3+Lw7yU3fPXe8FXpsqdLUy3TibaDD+28n9PguYjX5D4XrhMwxT6pbM8lEzz0T33xaUJezztXf9uo/KDJnt1Wro2dlRrAH/6Q7fCRencGnXJkeo6C9U5XCoBYJ22oFGknjpIHlsaS0ky28oMLrmIUKfLW8oMCxQ65J9zMC46OO21R83rwduzwwi9BCzEa/3dd0J1cPVJfZv2aQvHYzPwvlWJ7MWHdZJ6A/O6DkhY/LmtfrQTdnY3+JDIo+zEgo+WN1wbfVsVv9LbSYYnw8Ipz+ALMXlM9fjh6+9hesHYoYze1SC5ACoIXcUfyvFJZSx8nQGUIAPu/09bNPFiRzO4A8E7FhcgdgZS1GK+zKEXffzxQEQHcyfgWfP2GJspOa4S8yiCTwn5fNvtFwFMMj29KAA+ga0w6MwoREXRnLPjtfkBBTSqj3Oz8mpkRdh3UK2pHsVG3gEF1332xLZ8eq/nZskslxmXytshrHEcZ8PAljbcpuKsSpYjxz9BES1DuZxTAbEkYKP93PWnnVgi4i0Njzbwied2Oj6y/uuFXL9aaJ5KT4FwRCq4wgSXACUGGsxT/ltrUP
